## Document 072: 2026年四季度宏观经济展望：分化延续

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年四季度宏观经济展望：分化延续.pdf
Report source: 中证鹏元
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 34
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

李雪伟
lixw@cspengyuan.com
主要内容：
核心观点：三季度逆周期政策加码，经济顶压前行，产需结构虽有分化，新动
能持续壮大。展望四季度，外部形势复杂严峻，地缘博弈持续演变，主要经济
体货币政策同步收紧，全球流动性边际收敛。国内经济加快转型升级，结构 K
型分化加剧，内需仍然不足。2026 年实现全年区间增长目标的难度不大，宏观
政策仍需要“发力提效”，加快落实一揽子存量政策和增量政策，增强经济发
展动能，保障经济平稳运行。财政政策要持续提高专项债、新型政策性金融工
具、地方债结存限额等资金的使用进度，聚焦“六张网”建设，助力稳投资，
统筹全年收官与跨年项目储备。货币政策适度宽松，降息降准可期，结构性工
具精准发力，择机推动政策利率换锚，强化预期引导，稳定人民币汇率。房地
产延续“控增量、去库存、优供给”主线，加快房地产业新模式落地。
一、2026 年前三季度宏观经济回顾：总体平稳，结构向优，内外需分化
前三季度宏观经济整体平稳，实际 GDP 同比增长 4.6%，位于 4.5%-5.0%的目

标区间。供给端偏强，需求端结构分化，消费偏弱，制造业投资和基建投资在
政策发力下降幅收窄，地产投资低位徘徊，出口端保持高景气。输入性通胀影
响生产端价格水平持续改善，居民端温和修复。货币政策延续适度宽松，结构
性工具精准发力。财政政策保持积极，用好存量同时，适时推出增量政策，注
重投资于人，激发私人部门内生动力。深化拓展“人工智能+”，推动科技自立
自强、产业链自主可控。
二、2026 年四季度宏观经济展望：边际修复，新旧动能转换延续，实现“十五
五”良好开局
（一）外部环境：美日欧货币政策从分化走向同步收紧，中期选举将重塑美国
两党格局。美国经济 K 型分化格局延续，美联储 9 月重启加息，将维持货币限
制性政策。欧元区经济弱复苏，短期抗通胀优先于稳增长。日本经济面临高利
率压制和债务约束，央行将继续渐进加息。地缘政治博弈持续，中东冲突进入
拉扯阶段，美国中期选举将重塑两党格局。
（二）内部环境：经济转型再平衡，供强需弱延续，企业盈利分化。当前宏观
分化延续—2026年四季度宏观经济展望

宏观研究
2026年 10 月 21 日

### Page 2

经济新旧动能加速转换，K 型分化延续。总需求边际增量高度依赖公共部门外
生拉动，私人部门持续处于防御性修复期，微观主体资本开支意愿钝化。供需
错配延续，高技术产业链的局部繁荣对内循环的溢出拉动作用有限，内需偏弱
仍是核心制约。受地缘与能源价格带来的输入性通胀影响，PPI 中枢再次被动
抬升，但在终端需求不足制约下价格向下游传导受阻，中下游实体企业盈利空
间遭到实质性挤压。
（三）宏观经济边际修复，预计增长目标顺利实现，实现“十五五”良好开局。
预计四季度经济整体延续回升，全年 GDP实际增速在 4.7%左右，落在目标区间
中枢偏下位置。四季度 PPI可能高位徘徊但总体呈下降趋势。生产端在科技制
造景气引领下边际修复；出口有望维持高增速，内需环比弱修复，房地产市场
维持低位震荡格局，基建投资和制造业投资有望降幅收窄回稳。海外需关注美
国中期选举扰动与中欧贸易摩擦；国内重点关注一揽子增量政策下降准降息等
宽松工具的出台节奏，以及 10 月底五中全会，12 月政治局会议与中央经济工
作会议对明年经济工作与增长目标的定调。

（四）宏观政策：加快资金使用，择机推动政策利率换锚，纵深推进“人工智
能+”。宏观政策延续稳增长、培育新质生产力、促消费等方面的政策导向，四
季度大幅加码的必要性不强，但仍需要“发力提效”。财政政策加快资金使用
进度，聚焦“六张网”建设，积极稳投资，统筹全年收官与跨年项目储备。货
币政策延续适度宽松，择机推动政策利率换锚。纵深推进“人工智能+”行动，
加快现代化产业体系建设。

### Page 3

核心观点：三季度逆周期政策加码，经济顶压前行，产需结构虽有分化，新动能持续壮大。展望四季度，
外部形势复杂严峻，地缘博弈持续演变，主要经济体货币政策同步收紧，全球流动性边际收敛。国内经济加
快转型升级，结构 K 型分化加剧，内需仍然不足。2026年实现全年区间增长目标的难度不大，宏观政策仍
需要“发力提效”，加快落实一揽子存量政策和增量政策，增强经济发展动能，保障经济平稳运行。财政政
策要持续提高专项债、新型政策性金融工具、地方债结存限额等资金的使用进度，聚焦“六张网”建设，助
力稳投资，统筹全年收官与跨年项目储备。货币政策适度宽松，降息降准可期，结构性工具精准发力，择机
推动政策利率换锚，强化预期引导，稳定人民币汇率。房地产延续“控增量、去库存、优供给”主线，加快
房地产业新模式落地。
一、2026 年前三季度宏观经济回顾：总体平稳，结构向优，内外需分化
（一）前三季度实际 GDP 同比增长 4.6%，位于 4.5%-5.0%的目标区间
前三季度经济增速整体保持平稳。初步核算，前三季度我国 GDP 总量为 1,071,071 亿元，按不变价格

计算的实际 GDP 同比增长 4.6%。分季度看，一季度实际 GDP 同比增长 5.0%，二季度同比增长 4.3%，三
季度同比增长 4.5%。从环比看，经季节因素调整后，三季度 GDP 环比增长 1.2%，较二季度回升 0.3个百分
点。分产业看，前三季度第一产业增加值 58,994亿元，同比增长 3.7%；第二产业增加值 383,822亿元，同
比增长 3.8%；第三产业增加值 628,255 亿元，同比增长 5.2%，三大产业对经济增长的贡献率分别为 4.6%、
29.1%和 66.3%，第三产业贡献率较上年同期提高 5.6个百分点，三次产业增加值占 GDP的比重分别为 5.5%、
35.8%和 58.7%。三大需求方面，前三季度消费支出对经济增长贡献率为 46%，拉动 GDP 增长 2.1 个百分
点，较去年同期有所回落；资本形成总额对经济增长贡献率为 28%，拉动 GDP 增长 1.3 个百分点；货物和
服务净出口对经济增长贡献率为 26%，拉动 GDP 增长 1.2个百分点。前三季度，全国居民人均可支配收入

34,230元，比上年同期名义增长 5.3%，实际增长 4.4%。

### Page 6

（二）结构性就业压力较大，PPI 与 CPI 剪刀差走阔
前三季度，全国城镇调查失业率平均值为 5.2%，和上年同期持平；9 月份，全国城镇调查失业率为 5.2%，
比上个月下降 0.1个百分点。31 个大城市城镇调查失业率为 5.2%，和上年同期持平。分年龄段来看，9 月
末（不含在校生）16-24 岁、25-29 岁、30-59 岁劳动力调查失业率分别为 18.0%、7.4%、3.9%，较去年同期
整体抬升，青年人面临的就业压力仍较大，重点群体的就业支持政策仍需持续发力。2026 前三季度全国企
业就业人员周平均工作时间为 48.3小时，较 2025年前三季度同比下降 0.3 小时。

前三季度，居民消费价格 CPI 同比上升 0.9%，其中 9 月全国居民消费价格同比上涨 0.9%，环比上涨
0.2%。前三季度，扣除食品和能源价格后的核心 CPI 同比上涨 1.1%。分类别看，食品烟酒及在外餐饮价格
同比下降 0.4%，衣着价格上涨 1.5%，居住价格下降 0.2%，生活用品及服务价格上涨 1.4%，交通通信价格
上涨 2.0%，教育文化娱乐价格上涨 1.2%，医疗保健价格上涨 2.3%，其他用品及服务价格上涨 9.8%。在食
品烟酒及在外餐饮价格中，猪肉价格下降 12.9%，粮食价格下降 0.4%，鲜果价格上涨 0.7%，鲜菜价格上涨
1.4%； 9 月末猪肉平均批发价为 16.42元/公斤，较上半年低点 14.28元/公斤有所抬升。从三季度来看，地
缘政治导致国际原油价格上涨冲击全球物价水平，从年初的 60美元/桶一度飙升至 3 月末的 118.35 美元/桶，
6月均价为 84.43美元/桶，9 月均价为 102.02美元/桶，环比上涨 17.59美元/桶。

前三季度，PPI 比上年同期上涨 2.3%，涨幅比上半年扩大 0.8个百分点。分月看，1、2 月份 PPI 同比分
别下降 1.4%和 0.9%；3月份，中东地缘政治冲突推高国际原油价格，PPI 同比转为上涨 0.5%；输入性因素
传导叠加国内外需求增加，4 月份 PPI 同比涨幅进一步扩大至 2.8%；5-8 月份 PPI 同比涨幅在 3.5%-4.1%之
间；9 月份，受国际能源价格快速上涨等因素影响，PPI 同比上涨 5.0%。前三季度，人工智能等领域加速发
展，算力网、新一代通信网等加快建设，带动相关元器件及装备制造等行业需求增加、价格上涨。

（三）信贷降速提质新常态，政府债发力托底社融增量
前三季度社会融资规模增量累计为 27.46 万亿元，比上年同期少增 2.63 万亿元，低于近三年同期均值，
整体融资需求仍显不足。具体分项来看，政府债券净融资仍是支撑社融增长的重要因素，企业债券融资表现
较为强劲，而人民币贷款增长相对偏弱。前三季度政府债券净融资 10.30 万亿元，同比少增 1.16 万亿元；
企业债券净融资 2.75 万亿元，同比多增 1.18 万亿元；社融口径人民币贷款增加 11.50 万亿元，同比少增
3.04 万亿元。9 月末社会融资规模存量为 468.17 万亿元，同比增长 7.1%，较上月下降 0.1 个百分点；M2
同比增长 7.2%，较上月下降 0.3 个百分点；M1 同比增长 3.2%，较上月下降 0.9个百分点，M2 与 M1 剪刀
差较上月走阔 0.6个百分点至 4.0 个百分点，社融与 M2 增速差收窄至-0.1 个百分点，反映货币活化程度有

所减弱，实体经济有效融资需求仍有待提振。
前三季度信贷增加 11.52 万亿元，比上年同期少增 3.23 万亿元，信贷投放整体偏弱。分部门看，居民
端信贷需求持续低迷，前三季度居民贷款累计减少 1.03 万亿元，同比少增 2.13 万亿元，其中短期贷款减少
1.14 万亿元，同比少增 0.91 万亿元，反映居民短期消费及经营融资需求偏弱；中长期贷款增加 1,062亿元，
同比少增 1.22 万亿元，居民中长期融资需求仍显不足。企业端贷款增加 12.55 万亿元，同比少增 0.89 万亿
元，其中短期贷款增加 4.64 万亿元，同比多增 0.11 万亿元；中长期贷款增加 6.53 万亿元，同比少增 1.76
万亿元，企业中长期融资需求偏弱；票据融资增加 1.24 万亿元，同比多增 0.76 万亿元，反映信贷结构仍有
待改善。非银行业金融机构贷款减少 5,865亿元。总体来看，前三季度信贷投放呈现居民端持续承压、企业

### Page 11

中长期贷款偏弱、票据融资增加的结构性特征，实体经济内生融资需求仍有待进一步修复。

（四）供给端：工业生产季节性波动，现代服务业保持高景气扩张
供给端保持较快增长，前三季度全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.3%，增速较上半年有所减缓，其
中 7-9 月同比分别增长 4.5%、5.2%和 5.5%，工业生产增速逐月回升。装备制造业和高技术制造业增势强劲，
增加值同比分别增长 10.2%和 14.3%，分别高于全部规模以上工业增加值增速 4.9 和 9.0 个百分点，新动能
对工业增长的支撑作用持续增强。分三大门类看，制造业增加值同比增长 5.7%，高于全部规模以上工业增
加值增速 0.4个百分点；采矿业和电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业分别增长 2.0%和 5.4%。分经济类型

看，国有控股企业增加值同比增长 3.8%，股份制企业增长 5.8%，外商及港澳台投资企业增长 3.2%，私营企
业增长 4.3%。分产品看，3D 打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器人产品产量同比分别增长 62.5%、39.5%和

### Page 12

29.1%。制造业 PMI 前三季度围绕荣枯线波动，7-9 月分别为 49.2%、49.8%和 50.1%，9 月较上月回升 0.3
个百分点，重返扩张区间；其中生产指数为 51.7%，较上月上升 1.3个百分点，新订单指数为 50.5%，较上
月下降 0.1个百分点，生产端扩张有所加快，需求端修复仍有待巩固。1-8 月份，全国规模以上工业企业实
现利润总额 52,720 亿元，同比增长 15.7%，工业企业盈利修复动能放缓。
前三季度，服务业增加值同比增长 5.2%。其中，租赁和商务服务业，信息传输、软件和信息技术服务

比增长 4.8%，比上月加快 0.7 个百分点。其中，信息传输、软件和信息技术服务业，租赁和商务服务业，交
通运输、仓储和邮政业，住宿和餐饮业生产指数分别增长 9.8%、9.5%、5.4%、5.3%。9月份，服务业商务
活动指数为 50.2%，比上月上升 0.9个百分点；服务业业务活动预期指数为 55.5%，与上月持平。其中，电

月份，规模以上服务业企业营业收入同比增长 6.0%。

（五）需求端：消费修复动能仍显不足，出口保持高景气，投资降幅收窄
从消费来看，前三季度社会消费品零售总额 370,001亿元，同比增长 1.1%，增速较上半年回落 0.2个百
分点，消费增长整体偏弱。9 月社会消费品零售总额为 42,433 亿元，同比增长 1.1%，较上月加快 0.7 个百
分点，消费增速有所回升。具体来看，9 月商品零售同比增长 1.0%，较 8月加快 0.7个百分点；餐饮收入同
比增长 2.1%，较 8 月加快 1.0 个百分点，商品和餐饮消费均有所改善。消费市场整体低位运行，从结构上
看，汽车消费相对偏弱，三季度扣除汽车类商品后的消费品零售额同比增长 3.0%，较二季度加快 1.0 个百
分点；基本生活类商品保持一定韧性，前三季度限额以上单位粮油食品类、服装鞋帽针纺织品类和日用品类
商品零售额同比分别增长 6.8%、4.6%和 3.8%。消费品以旧换新政策持续推进，智能绿色消费表现较好，通

讯器材类和文化办公用品类商品零售额同比分别增长 18.1%和 6.2%。网络消费和乡村消费保持较快增长，
前三季度网上商品零售额 95,060 亿元，同比增长 3.8%，其中吃类、穿类、用类商品零售额分别增长 15.8%、
3.4%和 0.8%；按经营单位所在地看，前三季度城镇消费品零售额 319,820亿元，同比增长 0.9%，乡村消费
品零售额 50,181 亿元，同比增长 2.3%，乡村消费增速快于城镇 1.4个百分点。服务消费仍保持较快增长，
前三季度服务零售额同比增长 5.0%，高于同期商品零售额增速 4.0 个百分点。
从外贸来看，前三季度我国进出口总值 5.80 万亿美元，同比增长 23.8%。其中，出口端保持较高景气
度，出口 3.37万亿美元，同比增长 21.3%，较上半年增长 3.8个百分点，其中 7-9 月出口同比分别增长 23.9%、
24.9%和 36.3%，三季度出口增速逐月回升；进口 2.43 万亿美元，同比增长 27.5%，进口增速快于出口 6.2
个百分点，进出口保持较快增长。从贸易市场结构看，前三季度我国对共建“一带一路”国家进出口金额同

比增长 17.1%，对欧盟、韩国进出口金额分别同比增长 12.9%和 59.2%，对美国进出口金额 4,625.42 亿美元，

### Page 14

同比增长 8.6%，多元化市场布局持续深化。从经营主体看，前三季度我国国有企业、外商投资企业和私营
企业进出口金额分别同比增长 25.0%、26.3%和 24.0%，各类市场主体进出口均保持较快增长。从出口产品
结构看，前三季度机电产品出口同比增长 23.7%，电动汽车、锂电池等绿色产品出口分别增长 72.3%和 36.4%，
电子元件、电线电缆出口分别增长 70.0%和 25.3%，机电、绿色及新兴技术相关产品出口表现亮眼，出口结
构持续优化。
从投资来看，前三季度全国固定资产投资（不含农户）334,115亿元，同比下降 7.2%，较上半年降幅扩
大 1.5个百分点，投资整体持续承压。分领域看，前三季度基础设施投资同比下降 6.1%，较 2025 年同期回
落 7.6 个百分点；制造业投资同比下降 2.1%，较 2025 年同期回落 4.8个百分点；房地产开发投资同比下降
20.6%，较 2025年同期降幅扩大 5.9个百分点，三大领域投资增速较上半年均有所回落。前三季度全国新建
商品房销售面积 56,483 万平方米，同比下降 13.2%；新建商品房销售额 53,799 亿元，同比下降 13.9%，房

地产市场仍处于调整阶段。同时，投资结构仍存在一定亮点，知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.3%，拉动全部
投资增长 1.2个百分点；设备工器具购置投资同比增长 9.7%，拉动全部投资增长 1.6个百分点。高技术产业
投资同比增长 4.9%，其中高技术制造业和高技术服务业投资分别增长 4.0%和 6.6%，电子电路制造业、锂
离子电池制造业及集成电路制造业投资分别增长 58.7%、20.9%和 14.2%。重点基础设施领域投资表现较好，
互联网和相关服务业、航空运输业、水上运输业及电力供应业投资分别增长 46.7%、13.8%、13.5%和 11.2%。

二、2026 年四季度宏观经济展望：边际修复，新旧动能转换延续，实现“十五五”良好开局
（一）外部形势：美日欧货币政策从分化走向同步收紧，中期选举将重塑美国两党格局
美国经济 K型分化格局延续，美联储将维持货币限制性政策。二季度 GDP环比折年增速小幅下行 0.3pct

### Page 16

至 2.2%，主要受到个人消费支出和人工智能相关的私人投资拉动，其中私人投资对 GDP 增速的贡献较一季
度小幅回落，但仍处较高位置；政府支出下滑形成部分抵消，进口上升拖累净出口贡献。三季度非农就业回
落，平均时薪同环比增长小幅放缓，劳动力市场需求边际降温，但失业率处于低位，就业市场相对平衡；部
分行业因 AI 的替代效应出现结构性失业，不过尚未形成明显冲击。通胀方面，能源价格继续主导价格波动，
7-9 月 CPI 同比由 3.4%反弹至 3.7%，9 月核心 CPI 同比上行至 2.6%，高于市场预期，核心通胀粘性仍在。
展望后续，美国经济在财富效应支撑的居民消费、AI 资本开支扩张等带动下有望保持较强韧性，高油价对
经济的冲击相对有限，但持续的高利率也将抑制企业和居民信贷需求，房地产、传统制造业等利率敏感型行
业与 AI 产业的 K 型分化格局将延续。通胀方面，能源价格走势存在不确定性，其中原油价格有低库存和战
略储备回补需求的支撑，中东冲突存在反复，通胀中枢预计整体抬升，回落周期将拉长。货币政策上，美联

储 9 月启动加息，收回前期部分宽松。四季度通胀处于相对高位，较 2%的政策目标仍有距离，美联储将维
持货币限制性政策，基准情形下 12月或有一次加息，未来具体的加息路径和幅度仍主要取决于美伊冲突进
展和通胀走势。财政方面，虽然联邦赤字维持高位，但是为刺激经济、应对中期选举，美国财政扩张基调难
以系统性逆转。此外，《大而美法案》等前期政策形成的减税红利在 2026年达到峰值，若无新一轮财政刺
激，财政脉冲将由正转负，财政货币双重收紧对经济的拖累效应可能将逐步显现。
欧元区经济弱复苏，短期抗通胀优先于稳增长。欧元区经济弱复苏延续，面临“低增长、高通胀”的滞
胀压力。今年二季度实际 GDP 同比增长 1.2%，较一季度上升 0.6pct，主要拉动来自货物和服务出口，内需
消费增速平稳。随着不确定性消退、市场信心改善，三季度欧元区经济活动较二季度出现修复，9 月经济景
气指数回升至 97.9，ZEW 经济景气指数大幅上升至 25.8，中东冲突带来的负面冲击低于预期；欧洲消费者
信心边际增强，服务业 PMI 三季度重回扩张区间，欧元区消费者信心指数从 6 月的-17.7回升至 9 月的 16.5；

制造业保持高景气，欧元区制造业 PMI 连续 8 个月处于扩张区间，而且行业景气度较二季度上行。欧元区
各国经济分化，德国受益于国防支出、AI 建设而增长较快，法国受到极端天气的拖累。展望后续，作为能
源进口依赖型地区，欧洲仍面临俄乌战争、美伊冲突等地缘风险持续带来的外部不确定性，同时全球需求回
落、欧洲与中美贸易摩擦风险将压制出口动能；能源价格将沿产业链传导，对制造业产业链的长期结构性损
伤难以在短期内修复。财政方面，德国财政扩张力度最大，对经济拉动效应显著；法国、意大利等部分高债
务国财政压力仍存，赤字率维持高位，面临一定的债务可持续风险。欧元区经济依赖内生消费需求，难以实
现强劲反弹；相对于 2025 年 1.5%的增速，欧央行 9 月预测 2026年 GDP 增速为 0.9%，整体增速延续放缓
趋势。货币政策上，欧央行 6 月开启近三年来的首次加息，9 月再次加息，旨在确保通胀在中期内稳定在 2%
的目标水平，当前货币政策目标中抗通胀优先于稳增长。四季度油价或维持高位震荡，而且存在能源价格反

弹引发通胀二次传导的风险，欧央行通胀压力并未缓解，预计将保持货币政策紧缩状态，市场定价 12 月仍
有加息 25 个基点的可能。

### Page 17

日本经济面临高利率压制和债务约束，央行将继续渐进加息。日本经济呈现微弱复苏态势，二季度 GDP
环比折年率为 1.4%，连续三个季度正增长，但低于预期，内需疲软与外部需求的结构性分化成为核心特征，
人工智能的旺盛需求未能完全对冲内需的下滑。三季度日本制造业景气度保持高位，制造业 PMI 均处在扩
张区间，地缘扰动不改制造业景气的上升趋势。通胀方面，油价、海外大宗商品、日元贬值等推升进口成本，
劳动力紧平衡支撑薪资上行，日本 CPI 和核心 CPI 三季度上行；受益于政府补贴对能源和食品涨价的缓冲
效果，1-8 月均未超过 2%的通胀目标。四季度输入型通胀压力持续，政府补贴退坡导致电费、燃气费等价
格出现跳涨，没有新增政策的情况下通胀面临上行压力。货币政策方面，日本央行 9 月如期加息 25BP，将
政策目标利率提至近 31 年新高。行长植田和男最新表示核心通胀已经“十分接近”央行设定的 2%通胀目
标，存在进一步加息的空间。日本央行可能倾向避免连续加息，但不排除 12月再度加息的可能性。首相高

市早苗倾向于偏鸽派的货币政策以支持经济增长，支持扩张性财政刺激，如额外支出、结构性减税和补贴；
其上任以来推出多项支出方案，包括为期两年的食品消费税减免、面向战略产业的长期投资计划等。不过日
本货币政策受到央行独立性和通胀数据的制约，财政政策受限于高债务和刚性支出，实际扩张规模或相对
有限。整体来看，日本经济面临高利率压制和债务约束，日元贬值风险仍存，增长动力主要来自内需的温和
复苏和全球 AI 产业周期的拉动，GDP 预计保持低速增长；日本央行将继续推进渐进加息，未来半年内或有
一次加息。
地缘政治博弈持续，中东冲突进入拉扯阶段，美国中期选举将重塑两党格局。全球地缘局势呈现局部
冲突频发、重大战争可控化的特点，大国博弈进入相对稳定阶段。美伊双方进入边打边谈的拉扯阶段，以色
列间歇性干扰和谈进程；虽然美伊双方有意管控分歧，但双方分歧巨大，后续局部冲突难以避免，不确定性
仍存，中期选举后存在冲突升级的风险。在伊朗局势稳定后，美国或将目光转移至古巴等地区。俄乌冲突陷
入焦灼状态，期间有和谈信号释放，但和平进程较难推进。日本保守化倾向持续加深，高市早苗政府推动修

宪和军事松绑，持续在核问题边界试探，而且对华战略进一步向“全面防范优先”倾斜，东亚地区军备竞赛
风险增加。中美博弈进入“斗而不破”的阶段性平衡，难改长期竞争和博弈的本质；5 月元首峰会后两国进
入新的平台期，下半年延续高频沟通态势，给中美关系注入更多稳定剂。此外，11 月美国中期选举可能迎
来反转，民主党拿下两院的概率上升至六成。特朗普面临支持率低迷、民众生活成本高企等不利局面，美伊
冲突的焦灼局面消耗其政治根基。按照趋势，民主党在中期选举拿回众议院的概率较高，参议院归属主要看
少数摇摆州的选择。不过即使是民主党横扫两院，由于未掌握绝大多数，未来两年其对美国的影响主要在程
序层面而非立法，实质影响集中在拨款权，即民主党主导众议院时，后续军费与扩大战事的授权会明显受
限。两党博弈加剧，政府停摆、债务上限危机发生的风险上升。

（二）内部环境：经济转型再平衡，供强需弱延续
新旧动能加速转换，结构性分化延续。三季度，宏观经济结构调整阵痛延续，传统板块出清继续拖累总
需求。前三季度房地产开发投资完成额累计同比降幅扩大至 20.60%，并沿产业链向上压制原材料与中上游
工业需求。基础原材料行业 PMI 在三季度持续处于收缩区间，黑色金属冶炼及压延加工业、非金属矿物制
品业利润大幅下滑。科技制造主线则保持较强韧性，高技术制造业 PMI 持续运行在高景气区间。新旧动能
转换的非对称性在内外需中进一步凸显。外需受全球 AI 与算力周期驱动，集成电路出口同比增长 108.10%，

### Page 23

数据处理设备出口增长 54.60%，直接拉动电子设备制造业前八个月利润同比大增 109.90%。而传统板块及
劳动密集型产业链则持续承压，地产后周期陶瓷产品出口下降 27.20%，鞋靴出口亦录得 2.50%的负增长。
由外需和科技周期驱动的增量动能短期内难以完全弥补传统内需走弱的缺口，经济再平衡诉求更为凸显。
供需错配延续，内需仍然是核心制约。生产端在三季度整体“先抑后稳”，季初受极端天气及淡季扰动
阶段性承压，9月随着扰动消退及政策托底，工业增加值当月同比回升至 5.50%，制造业 PMI 生产指数反弹
至扩张区间。尽管季末生产企稳，但高技术产业链的繁荣对传统制造业及国内大循环的溢出拉动作用并不
显著。消费端商品弱、服务强特征延续，前三季度商品零售累计同比仅录得 1%，而服务零售同比增长 5%。
叠加地产周期拖累，9月建筑及装潢材料类零售额同比下跌 9.70%，整体内生消费动能尚未得到实质性扭转。
投资端当前面临的主要问题在于优质项目储备不足及实物工作量转化时滞。9月初“六张网”基建规划明确、

政府债券发行提速，政策性金融工具实质性投放，但核心工作量主要落在四季度，未能完全拉动三季度表
现，前三季度全国固定资产投资完成额累计同比仍下降 7.20%，仅 9 月制造业投资降幅收窄至 0.90%表现出
局部企稳迹象。
内需结构“公强私弱”，边际增量高度依赖公共部门外生拉动。从私人部门来看，居民端资产负债表持
续防御性修复。受收入预期下修约束，前三季度全国人均可支配收入实际累计同比录得 4.4%，虽有所抬升
但依然偏低，导致居民加杠杆意愿显著放缓，前三季度居民中长期贷款仅新增 1,062 亿元，同比大幅少增
12,238亿元。企业端受制于终端需求不足与资本回报率预期偏弱，资本开支意愿钝化，前三季度民间固定资
产投资累计同比大幅下探至-10.4%的历史低位，微观主体经营重心普遍转向降本增效与债务去化。与私人部
门信用收缩相对应，政府部门加杠杆权力托底，前三季度政府债券融资在社融增量中的占比达 37.5%。三季
度末，财政金融协同促内需持续加码，通过盘活并动用地方债务结存限额保障支出强度，新型政策性金融工

具开始实质性投放。同时，财政支出逻辑边际优化，向投资于人与直达终端倾斜，通过贷款贴息、定向补贴
等方式直接降低微观主体融资成本与刚性支出负担。
输入性通胀再次推高 PPI 读数，企业上下游盈利分化加剧。叠加全球地缘博弈与供给端约束，国际能
源价格高位运行成为扰动国内物价的核心外生变量。9 月以来，布伦特原油期货结算价再次突破 100 美元/
桶关口，直接抬升国内能源品价格，PPI 当月同比在 9 月冲高至 5.00%，带动前三季度 PPI 累计同比达 2.3%。
与此同时，受制于内生有效需求不足，核心 CPI 表现依然疲弱，9月当月同比仅录得 1.00%。PPI 与核心 CPI
剪刀差持续走阔，映射出上游成本抬升无法顺畅向下游传递的价格传导阻滞。此外，上游资源品行业直接受
益于输入性涨价逻辑，盈利表现强劲；反观中下游实体企业，则面临原材料成本高企与终端需求疲弱的双头
挤压，盈利遭到实质性侵蚀，8月汽车制造、家具制造等下游行业利润同比分别深跌 16.00%与 51.80%。这
种成本冲击导致中下游企业资本回报率持续承压，进一步压制了实体经济的扩表动能。

（三）宏观经济边际修复，预计增长目标顺利实现，实现“十五五”良好开局
四季度经济整体预计延续回升，全年经济增速在 4.7%左右，实现“十五五”良好开局。四季度全球经
济和贸易形势仍趋复杂严峻，地缘政治冲突持续，能源价格持续高位加剧通胀和供应链风险，主要经济体进

### Page 27

入加息周期。国内经济延续 K 型分化，新旧动能持续转换，外需和高端制造业托举大盘，内需虽有改善但
变化不明显。四季度国内宏观政策仍需积极发力，进一步扩内需和稳经济，预计全年 GDP 实际增速在 4.7%
左右，基本落在 4.5%-5.0%目标区间的中间偏下位置。价格水平方面，受结构性油价和输入型通胀影响，9
月 PPI 再次冲涨，进一步压缩中下游利润；四季度 PPI 可能高位徘徊，但总体价格水平将呈现高点下降趋
势，后续需重点关注中东局势和油价变化。四季度国内重点关注一揽子增量政策下，降准降息等总量型宽松
工具的出台节奏，聚焦 10 月底五中全会，以及 12 月政治局会议和中央经济工作会议对明年经济工作与增
长目标的定调。
生产端边际修复，新质生产力延续高景气引领。三季度极端天气与制造业传统淡季的扰动后，叠加增量
政策的托底效应，四季度生产端有望迎来边际修复。新旧动能转换稳步推进，科技制造主线动能强劲。在出
口产业链拉动与全球 AI 算力周期的共振下，以半导体及高端电子设备为主导的高技术制造业持续繁荣，对

工业供给大盘形成坚实支撑。但与此同时，工业生产内部的结构性分化依然显著。科技制造的高景气对传统
制造产业链的外溢带动作用相对有限，传统高载能行业与部分中下游加工制造仍面临内需偏弱与成本压制
的双重约束。四季度生产端的全面提质增效，仍有赖于增量政策向终端有效需求的加速传导。
出口有望继续维持高增速，内需环比弱修复。出口方面，受低基数效应和全球 AI 周期的持续拉动，四
季度出口有望维持 20%以上的高增速，继续充当宏观大盘的核心托举力量，但出口内部同样存在显著的结
构性分化。国内大循环方面，内需预计环比有所修复，但居民预期和微观体感依然不强，总体仍偏弱，尤其
是消费低迷和房地产投资下行的拖累仍在延续。后续促消费的重点需放在大力发展服务消费、拓展消费亮
点，并切实提高居民的收入预期上。投资端，固定资产投资有望降幅收窄并逐步回稳。9 月初启动的“六张
网”规划、政府债加速发行及政策性金融工具投放，其实物工作量将集中在四季度兑现，带动基建与制造业
投资边际企稳。房地产投资端，开发投资、新开工及销售等核心指标仍在低位徘徊，短期难言实质性反转，

但政策密集催化已带来预期拐点，预计后续修复过程大概率将延续城市间的分化特征，不同能级城市的复
苏节奏与力度将进一步拉开差距。9 月一线城市新房单月同比增速转正，10 月初京沪深等核心城市二手房
成交放量反弹，活跃度超过去两年同期。随着首套房财政贴息政策落地，四季度有望脉冲式撬动中小户型刚
需入市，对成交形成边际托底。预计四季度房地产市场整体维持弱复苏、强分化的低位震荡格局，但需警惕
二手房“以价换量”对价格体系的持续压制。

（四）宏观政策：聚焦扩内需和增量举措
宏观政策延续稳增长、培育新质生产力、促消费等方面的政策导向，投资端以“六张网”和重大项目形
成实物工作量，金融端以补充银行资本和政策性金融工具打开信用扩张空间，消费端以财政贴息和服务业
扩能提质双向发力，着力优化经济结构与提升政策效能。在“努力完成全年经济社会发展目标任务”的要求
下，四季度实际经济增速最低只需达到 4.3%左右，即可支撑全年增速达到 4.5%的下限，宏观政策大幅加码
的必要性不强，但仍需要“发力提效”，加快落实一揽子存量政策和增量政策，增强经济发展动能，同时及

### Page 29

时出台储备政策，确定经济平稳运行。
货币政策：先结构后总量，择机推动政策利率换锚。三季度央行延续适度宽松的政策立场，逆周期调节
加码，结构性工具精准发力；隔夜逆回购操作常态化，DR001 围绕政策利率中枢波动，短端利率稳定性显
著提升。央行未出台总量政策，而是通过 MLF 加量续作、隔夜逆回购扩容、PSL 降息扩围、两项再贷款增
额四条路径定向发力，同时在需求端配合财政部门推出居民购房贷款贴息。展望后续，经济处于新旧动能转
换的阵痛期，货币政策继续保持支持性立场，为内需回暖和产业转型创造适宜的货币金融环境。货币政策延
续“先结构后总量”，年内降准降息仍可期。结构性工具继续强化，加力支持扩内需、科技创新、中小微企
业等重点领域。隔夜利率锚定强化，择机推动政策利率换锚，深化货币价格型调控。设立应对非银机构大额
赎回的流动性支持工具，及时为机构提供流动性托底。强化预期引导，稳定人民币汇率，防范跨境资本波动
冲击国内流动性环境。
财政政策：加快存量政策落地，统筹全年收官与跨年项目储备。为实现“十五五”良好开局，财政政策

作为逆周期核心抓手，需要加快资金使用和政策协同，统筹全年收官与跨年项目储备。具体来看：（1）总
量政策继续落实“更加积极”的主基调，广义财政保持高强度；用好用足存量政策，根据需要及时储备增量
政策，如提前下达 2027年新增地方债限额，延续两项年底到期的消费贴息政策，或追加相关预算安排；（2）
加快资金的使用进度。加紧推动 109项重大工程、“六张网”项目、“两重”建设进度，持续提高专项债、
新型政策性金融工具、地方债结存限额等资金的使用进度，项目端和资金端齐发力，撬动更多社会资本，尽
早形成实物工作量。（3）支出结构继续优化，坚持投资于物和投资于人并重：一方面投向新型能源、算力
基础设施、产业链补短板工程；另一方面持续加码民生、公共服务和就业扶持，提升教育、医疗、社保、养
老等支出占比，夯实内循环基础。（4）落实防风险和基层保障，兜牢“三保”底线，多措并举推进化债攻
坚，持续压降地方隐性债务，严控新增。（5）加强财政贴息与结构性货币工具、专项债资本金与银行配套
融资的协同配合，保持财政政策、货币政策和产业政策取向一致性，促进政策同向发力。（6）统筹全年收

官与跨年项目储备：超前谋划一批符合政策导向、前期工作扎实的优质项目，为明年财政起好步赢得先机。
产业政策：纵深推进“人工智能+”行动，加快现代化产业体系建设。围绕科技自立自强和新质生产力
培育，政策以“人工智能+”为核心抓手，优化传统产业和新兴产业布局，以科技创新引领新质生产力发展，
以深化“反内卷”来规范市场秩序，构建智能化、绿色化、融合化的现代产业体系。具体看：一是纵深推进
“人工智能+”行动，加快人工智能商业化规模化应用和生态建设，全方位赋能千行百业；分层推进六大新
兴支柱产业规模化，强化产业链上下游协同、区域错位布局。二是前瞻布局未来产业，完善投入增长与风险
分担机制，加大基础研究投入，抢占技术制高点。三是加码传统产业智能化绿色化改造，落实设备更新和节
能降碳补贴，推动钢铁、化工等重点行业产能置换以及能效升级；稳步推进新型能源体系建设，把握能源安
全主动权。四是持续落地“反内卷”和统一大市场配套政策，建立重点行业产能监测预警，通过标准、能耗、

### Page 30

准入引导供需平衡；分层分类壮大现代服务业，培育“中国服务”品牌，促进生产性服务业和先进制造业融
合。
房地产政策：延续“控增量、去库存、优供给”主线，加快房地产业新模式落地。2026年前三季度，
全国房地产市场延续总量寻底、结构分化的格局，核心一二线城市在政策加持下释放改善性需求，核心地段
项目韧性更强，二手房成交持续优于新房；三四线城市库存压力高、去化缓慢；全国房企新开工、拿地意愿
整体偏弱，地产投资同比跌幅尚未收敛，居民资产负债表修复进程缓慢。政策主线延续“控增量、去库存、
优供给”，同时增量政策取得突破：“929”房产政策为中央财政首次为房贷贴息，标志着房地产调控政策
从间接调控转为直接补贴，和“828”现房销售政策分别从需求端和供给端发力，短期将提振刚需成交，中
长期将加速供给侧出清，推动小户型房价早日止跌企稳。为更好适应房地产市场新格局，加快构建房地产发
展新模式，需要从供需两端继续发力。一方面，持续盘活存量，加大存量商品房收购转化保障房和安置房力
度，用好配套专项债；供地结构持续向核心区、好房子项目倾斜，严控远郊低效新增供地；优化房企融资支

持，保交房白名单动态扩容，落实资金封闭监管，优先保障已售项目交付。另一方面，因城施策继续优化限
制性政策，支持核心城市适时调整核心区限购、限贷；增加公积金贷款、购房补贴、存量房贷利率、交易税
负等方面的优惠力度，支持住房“以旧换新”，激活购房刚需和改善性需求；引导更多城市加快公积金政策
优化调整，提高贷款额度，扩大提取和缴存覆盖范围；针对初婚初育家庭、多子女家庭、青年人才等特殊群
体，推出差异化购房补贴、公积金贴息等政策，支持其住房需求。此外，“929”贴息后的房贷利率和公积
金贷款利率差异不大，居民购房成本边际下降，调降公积金贷款利率的时间预计推迟，短期出台的概率可能
不高。

## Document 101: 261027_daiwa_TSMT

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_TSMT.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Background: We met with Taiwan Surface Mounting Technology (TSMT) 
on 27 October. According to management, its product mix for 3Q26 was 
TFT-LCD 47%, Mini LED 9%, DRAM module 15%, auto 10%, cable 7%, 
touch 4% and others 8%. TSMT is positive on its optical transceiver, DRAM 
module, auto, low-earth orbit satellites (LEO), server and robotic segments. 
The QFII holding in TSMT was 29.7% on 22 October vs. 19.8% at the end 
of 2025.

Highlights: 4Q26 and 2027 outlook update – an upward revision. 
TSMT guided for its 4Q26 revenue to grow by 5-8% QoQ, and 2027 
revenue to grow by at least 20% (some upside potential subject to raw 
material conditions) for 2027 thanks to multiple revenue drivers, including 
optical transceivers, DRAM module, auto, LEO, server and robotic 
segments. For 4Q26 and 2027, the company expects its quarterly revenue 
and gross margin to further expand sequentially on a more favourable 
product mix from optical transceiver, LEO and server revenue. TSMT 
expects its gross margin to improve to 15-17% in 1H27 and over 20% in 
2H27 on the solid optical transceiver order outlook.

Strong revenue and margin outlook is expected to be driven mainly 
by the optical transceiver segment. TSMT mentioned several revenue 
drivers for 2027-28; among them, optical transceiver surface mounting 
technology (SMT) assembly is the most significant one. TSMT is a major 
global supplier for 800G and 1.6T products. A US-based client has noted 
that the 1.6T delivery schedule was ahead of its previous expectation (from 
4Q26 to 3Q26). The company has thus won an additional US-based tier-
one client (expected to have a larger contribution than the first optical client 
from 3Q27) and a China-based client over the past few months. TSMT is 
also in talks with a new US-based tier-one optical transceiver client. 
According to TSMT, optical transceiver revenue will likely account for 5-
10% of total revenue in 1Q27, 10-15% in 2Q27 and 15-20% in 3Q27; for 
2028, the company aims for 30-40%. Given the pure outsourcing (ie, 
includes no buying and selling of material) nature of the optical transceiver 
business, the segment brings upside potential to the gross margin level, 
per management. Aside from the optical transceiver segment, the company 
also sees strong revenue momentum in 2027-2028 from the DRAM module 
(2027 revenue up 30+% YoY), auto, robotic, LEO and server segments. 
TSMT says it has been expanding its global capacity and adjusting its 
equipment to fit different usages, eg, transforming from TFT-LCD to optical 
transceiver.

Valuation: The stock is currently trading at PERs of 24.8x/13.8x, based on 
Bloomberg’s 2026E/27E EPS forecasts.

27 October 2026

주가 추이

3Q26 매 출액 과 영 업 이익 은 비현 금 성 비용 항목 개 선 으로 시장 기대치
를 상 회 했 으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인 플 레 이션 등으로 연간 가이 던 스 는 
하 향 되 었 습니 다. 올해 생산 차질 에 따라 이 연된 신차 판 매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시 화 하 는 부분 에 주목 필 요 합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 Y oY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제 외 하 면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년 으 로 이연된 것 이 라 는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본 격 화 된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로 컬 업 체 들 의 공세를 방 어 하 는 모 습 을 
확인하는 것이 가 능 할 것으로 판 단 한 다. 노 후 차 종 중심 운 영 에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근 원 적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 30일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180 억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK 을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

## Document 230: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업.pdf

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중

## Document 265: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

### Page 4

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 2-6
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 31 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 0.6% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 2.0%, both
posting their third straight week of losses, as disappointing tech earnings
revived concerns about AI-related spending.
The FOMC held the federal funds rate (FFR) at 3.75%-4.00% on
Wednesday in a 10-2 vote, with the dissenters favoring a 25bps hike. We
believe the committee needs another 50bps of increases to get back to a
neutral FFR that is neither accommodative nor restrictive.

October’s employment report (Fri) caps a jam-packed week of labor
market data, including JOLTS (Tue), ADP (Wed), and Challenger and
jobless claims (Thu). ISM’s M-PMI (Mon) and NM-PMI (Wed) are also
due. Six Fed officials are scheduled to speak. Tuesday’s midterm
elections should be interesting. Rate decisions from the Reserve Bank of
Australia and the Bank of England and earnings reports from Apple,
Palantir, AMD, and Qualcomm round out the week's calendar. Here’s

more:
(1) Employment. October’s employment report is the economic headliner.
Payrolls rose 29,000 in September, lowering the three-month average to
50,700 (chart). The unemployment rate rose to 4.2% from 4.1%, while
average hourly earnings increased just 0.1% m/m. We expect a stronger
payroll gain than the consensus, of approximately 55,000.
Challenger’s October layoff announcements (Thu) follow September’s
count of 43,300. Technology led September’s announced cuts, at 10,800.

Initial unemployment claims (Thu) remain low, with a four-week average of

### Page 4

197,800 for the week ended October 24 (chart). We expect more of the
same for both labor market indicators this week.
The four-week moving average of ADP's employment measure peaked at
26,000 in late September before easing to 16,750 in early October (chart).
September’s monthly ADP report showed a 90,000 gain in private
payrolls, nearly double the Bureau of Labor Statistics’ 46,000.
September’s JOLTS report (Tue) follows August’s 7.1 million job openings,

down from 7.3 million in July. The share of consumers saying jobs are
“plentiful” was 26.7% in October, little changed from 27.0% in August
(chart).

### Page 5

(2) PMIs. ISM’s M-PMI (Mon) follows September’s 54.5 reading, with the
prices-paid index at 77.9. The NM-PMI (Wed) was 54.9 (chart). S&P
Global’s flash M-PMI edged up to 56.0 in October from September’s 55.9,
a 53-month high, while the flash NM-PMI eased to 57.9 from 58.8. Both
still point to solid ISM readings.
S&P 500 forward earnings rose 38.8% y/y as of October 26, up from
37.2% in September, suggesting more upside for the M-PMI (chart).

### Page 6

(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season continues this week, with
31% of the S&P 500 companies set to report. Apple, Palantir, AMD, Arista
Networks, Qualcomm, and Arm headline the list.
Analysts’ consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for
S&P 500 companies of 23.8% y/y, down slightly from 24.1% last week,
after Alphabet, Amazon, and Intel missed estimates (chart). Q4 growth
estimates held at 30.2%.
(4) Global. The Reserve Bank of Australia (Tue) is expected to hike its

cash rate target by 25bps for a second straight meeting, to 4.85% from
4.60% (chart). The markets are pricing in 88% odds of that result, so it’s
no sure thing. Last week, the Reserve Bank of New Zealand held its
official cash rate at 2.75% despite 95% odds of a hike. Both central banks
are often seen as bellwethers for the global rate cycle.

### Page 7

The Bank of England (Thu) looks set to raise its rate by 25bps to 4.00%,
with 98% odds priced into the markets. The markets see a 35% chance of
another hike in December.
The Eurozone’s flash CPI (Wed) is expected to rise in October from
September’s 3.8% y/y, with the core rate holding at 2.5%. Among the
major economies, Spain’s headline rate was the highest at 5.0% in
September and Germany’s the lowest at 3.3% (chart).

Great improvement in 3Q26

with Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 gross margin of 43% was 2ppts
higher than Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 EPS of US$0.6 is much

US$1.1bn.
Data center IC sales beat: 3Q26 Data center IC sales (largely
server CPU sales) were US$7.32bn, up 17% qoq and 78% yoy,
nearly 10% above consensus estimates. Data center IC sales

accounted for 44% of 3Q26 sales, up from 2Q26’s 39% and from

4Q26 guidance missing on sales, margin, and EPS

memory price hike and PC customers to aggressively manage

capacity (limited by in house capacity after Israel Kiryat Gat fab
factory shut down), Intel guides 4Q26 sales of US$15-16bn, down 3-
9% qoq and 9% below Bloomberg estimate of US$17bn (Fab shut

deteriorate to 37% (Fab shut down aﬀects 2ppts margin), 6ppts
lower than 3Q26’s 43% and Bloomberg’s 42.8%, resulting much
lower diluted EPS of US$0.1 (Bloomberg’s US$0.3).

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

Figure  7-1 Final Demand and Intermediate Demand
Price Indexes (Goods)
Figu

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.
Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026

The Japan Research Institute, Limited- ８ -

フィリピン中銀、原油高とペソ安の「二重苦」に直面し、大幅利上げに舵 
～インフレ圧力の高まりを受けて利上げ幅を拡大、金融引き締めの長期化も～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ フィリピン中銀は、10 月 22 日の定例会合で政策金利を50bp 引き上げ、5.50％とすることを
決定した。４会合連続の利上げとなり、今回は利上げ幅を拡大するなど、金融引き締めの度
合いを強化した。背景には、イラン情勢の悪化に伴う原油高や、金融市場における通貨ペソ
安の進行を受けてインフレ圧力が強まったことがある。９月のインフレ率は前年比＋7.2％と
中銀の目標を大幅に上回っている。中銀はインフレ率が 2027 年も高水準で推移し、2028 年
に目標中央値の３％に近付くとの見通しを示した。レモロナ総裁は、今回の利上げが為替安
定を目的とするものではないと強調する一方、追加利上げや緊急利上げの可能性を排除しな
い姿勢を示した。先行きもイラン情勢の不透明感やペソ安圧力が続く可能性があり、さらな
る金融引き締めを迫られる展開も予想される。

フィリピン中央銀行は、10 月 22 日に開催した定例の金融政策委員会において、政策金利である
翌日物リバースレポ金利を 50bp 引き上げ、5.50％とすることを決定した。同行は、４月の定例会
合で約２年半ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、その後も６月、 ８月の定例会合で追加利上げを実施してお
り、今回で４会合連続の利上げとなる。さらに、今回は利上げ幅を拡大させるなど金融引き締めの
度合いを強めており、政策金利も2025 年６月以来の高い水準となった。 
背景には、イラン情勢の悪化による原
油高が世界的なエネルギーインフレを招
くなか、同国は原油需要の９割以上を輸
入に依存しており、その影響に晒されや
すいことがある。直近９月のインフレ率
は前年比＋7.2％と中銀の目標レンジ （２
～４％）を大きく上回っている（図１） 。
さらに、原油などエネルギー資源価格の
高止まりは輸入額の増加を通じて対外収
支を悪化させており、金融市場では通貨
ペソの対ドル相場が最安値を更新するな

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
ど、輸入物価の押し上げがインフレ圧力を一段と増幅させる懸念も高まっている。このため、中銀
としては物価安定に向けた『より強力な』対応を示す必要があるとの判断から、今回は市場予想を
上回る大幅利上げに舵を切ったと考えられる。 
会合後に公表した声明文では、 先行きの物価動向について 「今後数ヵ月は一段の加速が見込まれ
る」としたうえで、 「インフレ率は2027 年も高水準で推移し、2028 年には目標の中央値 （３％） に
近付く」 との見通しを示した。 さらに、 「コアインフレ率も上昇が予想され、 潜在的なインフレ圧力
の高まりを示唆している」 との見方を示した。 その理由として、 「イラン情勢の再燃による原油価格
の上昇」 、 「交通運賃の上昇によるインフレの上振れリスクが高まっている」ほか、 「エルニーニョ
現象による農業生産や価格へのリスク」を挙げた。一方で、景気動向について「先行きは財政支援
による回復が見込まれる」との見通しを示した。 
今回の決定については 「インフレ圧力の高まりと、 さらなる上振れリスクに対して強力な政策対
応の必要性を認識した」との考えを示した。先行きについて「今回の対応が金融環境を適切に抑制
的なものにする」 、 「副次的な影響を含めてインフレ期待をしっかりと抑える」 との見通しを示した。
また、先行きの政策運営については「物価安定という主要任務に基づいて、インフレ率を３％に戻
すべくあらゆる措置を講じる用意がある」 と過去数会合と同じ文言を載せており、 追加利上げに含
みを持たせた。 
会合後の記者会見に臨んだレモロナ総裁は、 物価見通しについて 「これまでの想定に比べて悪い」
との見方を示した。今回の決定を受けた政策スタンスについて「抑制的であるが、十分に抑制的で
あるかは定かでない」 と追加利上げに含
みを持たせた。 今回の利上げについて
「ペソ相場の安定を目的としたもので
はない」 、 「われわれは多少為替介入を実
施しているが、 相場の乱高下を防ぐこと
を目的としたもの」として、為替安定の
ための利上げではないと付言した。 とは
いえ、 「ペソ安はインフレを招く恐れが
あり、懸念している」と述べるなど、そ
の動向を注視する姿勢をみせた （図２） 。
先行きの政策運営について 「さらなる利
上げの可能性を排除しない」、「利上げ幅については 25bp か 50bp、それ以上かは言えない」としつ
つ、 「緊急利上げの必要性も議論された」 とした。こうした姿勢は、 中銀が切迫した状況に直面して
いることを示唆している。イラン情勢は見通しが立ちにくく、ペソ相場についても下落圧力に晒さ
れる展開が続く可能性があり、その動向には注意が必要である。

## Document 519: ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか ～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低_ba60bdfd

Source PDF: all report pdf/ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか ～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低_ba60bdfd.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 26 日

1/4 
ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか 
～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低成長下でも高金利が長期化する公算～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ロシア中銀は10 月 23 日の会合で、政策金利を２会合連続で 14.00％に据え置いた。2025 年
６月から 10 会合連続で利下げを進めてきたものの、インフレ再加速の兆しを受けて警戒姿
勢を維持した。９月のインフレ率は前年比＋6.35％、コアインフレ率も同＋5.47％で、とも
に目標の４％を上回る。中銀は 2026 年末のインフレ見通しを＋7.1～7.6％に上方修正し、
2027 年の平均政策金利見通しも12.5～13.5％へ引き上げるなど利下げ余地は縮小している。 
○ インフレ再燃の主因は国内の供給制約である。ウクライナによる製油所へのドローン攻撃で
石油精製量が減り、 全土でガソリン不足と価格上昇が起きているほか、10 月の公共料金引き
上げも物価を押し上げている。原油高による輸出収入がルーブルを支える一方、国内の燃料
高は解消せず、期待インフレや企業の価格見通しは高止まりしている。中銀は、供給ショッ
クが価格設定や賃金交渉に織り込まれる二次的波及を防ぐ狙いがあるとみられる。 
○ 景気面では、2026 年の成長率見通しは＋0.5～1.0％と小幅に上方修正されるも低水準にとど
まり、2027 年は＋1.0～2.0％へ下方修正された。人手不足など供給能力の制約から、低成長
下でも物価上昇圧力が残りやすく、景気の弱さを理由にした追加利下げのハードルは高まっ
ている。財政面でも、2027 年度予算案を中銀は慎重と評価する一方、歳出拡大を可能にする
法律が成立しており、追加歳出が利下げ余地をさらに狭める可能性がある。 
○ ナビウリナ総裁は引き締め姿勢の長期維持が必要との見方を示し、2027 年には利下げ余地が
生じ得るとしつつも、 インフレが想定より高止まりする可能性にも言及した。 次回会合は12
月 18 日に予定されている。供給制約の緩和と期待インフレの低下が進めば、利下げ再開の余
地は広がるものの、供給障害が長引けば据え置きが長期化するとみられる。

【ロシア中銀は２会合連続で利下げ休止を継続、インフレへの警戒姿勢を維持】 
ロシア中央銀行は、10 月 23 日に開催した定例会合において、政策金利を２会合連続で14.00％
に据え置くことを決定した。中銀は 2025 年６月から 2026 年７月まで 10 会合連続で利下げを実施
し、政策金利を21.00％から14.00％まで引き下げてきた。しかし、９月の前回会合では11 会合ぶ

### Page 2

2/4 
りに利下げを休止しており（注１）、今回も据え置きを継続した。 
背景には、2025 年初旬を境に鈍化して
きたインフレ に再加速の兆しが出てい
ることがある （図１） 。９月のインフレ率
は前年同月比＋6.35％となり、 前月の同
＋6.33％からほぼ横ばいとなった もの
の、中銀目標 （４％） を上回る伸びで推
移しており、 過去数ヵ月は緩やかに加速
の動きを強めている。 コアインフレ率は
前年同月比＋ 5.47 ％ と 前 月 （ 同 ＋
5.37％） から伸びが加速しており、 ５月
を境に加速に転じている。 
こうした状況を踏まえて、 中銀は2026 年末のインフレ率見通しを＋7.1～7.6％と従来見通し （＋
6.0～7.0％） から上方修正している。なお、2027 年末にはインフレ率が目標の４％に回帰する見通
しを維持しているものの、その実現に必要となる 2027 年の政策金利の平均水準を 12.5～13.5％と
従来見通し（10.5～12.5％）から引き上げており、先行きの利下げ余地が縮小していると捉えられ
る。その意味では、物価上昇圧力が残るなかで金融引き締め的な状態を長く維持する姿勢を示した。

【供給制約による燃料価格の上昇と期待インフレの高止まりが金融緩和の制約要因に】 
足元でインフレが再燃している背景には、 国内における供給制約が高まっていることがある。ウ
クライナによる製油所をはじめとするエネルギー・輸送インフラへのドローン攻撃 の強化を受け、
ロシア国内では石油精製量が減少し、ガソリンや軽油などの供給が制約されている。政府は、備蓄
分のガソリン放出のほか、 規制撤廃による低品質燃料の販売許可、 軽油の輸出禁止などにより国内
供給を優先させている。 にもかかわらず、 石油製品の需給がひっ迫して全土でガソリン不足が深刻
化して価格が押し上げられているほか、10 月１日からの公共料金の引き上げも物価を押し上げて
いる。燃料高は企業の輸送費などを通じて他の財・サービスにも波及する。中銀は、一部業種にお
ける生産能力の縮小を一時的な要因と位置付けつつ、 その物価への影響が想定より長期化する可能
性を警戒している。

注１ ９月14 日付レポート「ウクライナの攻勢によるガソリン不足がロシア中銀の利下げにブレーキ」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/655069.html）

### Page 3

3/4 
資源輸出国であるロシアにとって、原
油高の影響は複雑である。中銀は、原油
などの輸出収入の増加が最近のルーブ
ル相場を支えたと説明している（図２）。
ルーブル相場の安定は、輸入物価の抑制
要因となる。一方で、前述の通りロシア
国内においては、製油所の操業障害が石
油製品の供給を制約している。このた
め、足元のロシアにおいては、資源輸出
収入の増加 が対外収支の改善を促して
いるものの、国内の燃料高が併存する状
況に直面しており、インフレ圧力の解消は難しい状況にある。こうした事態を反映して、足元の期
待インフレ率や、 企業部門による価格見通しは高止まりしている。企業の価格設定や賃金交渉に高
いインフレが織り込まれ、 一時的な供給ショックの影響が長引くおそれがある。 今回中銀が政策金
利を据え置いた背景には、 需要の拡大を抑えながら、 こうした二次的な波及を防ぐ狙いがあると考
えられる。

【低成長の下で高金利の長期化を迫られるなど、政策運営のハードルは高まっている】 
足元の景況感は、サービス業を中心に底堅い動きが確認されるとともに、先行きの投資活動の回
復を示唆する動きもみられる （図３） 。 し
かし、 中銀が示した最新の経済成長率見
通しによれば、2026 年の経済成長率は＋
0.5 ～1.0 ％ と 従 来 見 通 し （ ＋0.0 ～
1.0％）から小幅に上方修正されたもの
の、 依然として低い伸びにとどまる。 さ
らに、2027 年も＋1.0～2.0％と従来見通
し （＋1.5～2.5％） から下方修正されて
いる。 足元では人手不足感が徐々に緩和
する兆しがみられるものの、 依然として
広範な分野で人手不足の懸念は残る。 こ
のように供給能力が制約されるなかでは、低成長のもとでも物価上昇圧力が残りやすいことから、
景気の弱さだけを理由に利下げを進めることのハードルは高まっていると考えられる。 
財政運営面では、政府が９月末に連邦議会下院（国家院）に提出した 2027 年度予算案について
（注２）、中銀は７月会合時点での想定に比べてやや慎重と評価したうえで、インフレ抑制に寄与

注２ 10 月２日付レポート「ロシア、財政運営はウクライナ戦争のさらなる長期化を鮮明に」

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
するとの見方を示している。しかし、今年６月に連邦議会は予算法の改正を経ない形で歳出、債務
の拡大を可能にする法律を可決しており、 国防費をはじめとする歳出を機動的に増加させることが
可能となっている。 政府によれば、2026 年だけを対象とする特例措置としたものの、プーチン政権
を支える与党・統一ロシアは議会の３分の２を超える議席数を獲得しており、先行きもなし崩し的
に同様の対応が進む可能性には注意が必要である。このため、 追加歳出などによって財政赤字が計
画を上回れば、需要を押し上げ、利下げ余地をさらに狭める可能性がある。財政健全化の計画が着
実に実行されるか否かの行方についても、金融政策の先行きを左右することが予想される。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだ中銀のナビウリナ総裁は、 当面の政策運営について 「長期間にわたっ
て引き締め姿勢を維持する必要がある」との見方を示したうえで、今回の決定について「据え置き
案のみを実質的に議論した」 と述べた。 先行きについて 「2027 年には利下げ余地が生まれる可能性
がある」 とする一方、 「インフレは想定に比べて高止まりするかもしれない」 と述べるなど、 その余
地が縮小しているとの認識を示す慎重姿勢をみせた。高金利の長期化は、 借り入れを通じた需要拡
大を抑える一方、 民間企業の資金調達や設備投資の負担にもなる。 供給能力の回復や生産性の向上
には投資が必要であり、 インフレ抑制を優先する政策と中長期の成長力確保との調整は難しい。 今
回の予測改定は、 物価目標への回帰に向けて、 景気回復のペースにも慎重な見方が必要になってい
ることを示すものと考えられる。 
次回の金融政策決定会合は12 月 18 日に予定されている。今後、生産能力の回復が進み、基調的
な物価上昇率と期待インフレが持続的に低下すれば、 利下げ再開の余地は広がると考えられる。一
方で、供給障害が長引き、公共料金や燃料価格の上昇が他の物価に波及すれば、据え置きが長期化
すると予想される。2027 年の平均政策金利見通しは現行の 14％を下回っており、中銀は緩和余地
を残しているものの、その時期や幅は物価動向次第である。今後の焦点は、供給制約の緩和が期待
インフレの低下につながり、 景気回復を支える利下げの余地をどこまで広げられるかにかかってい
る。 
以 上

（https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666237.html）

景気は弱い動き
中国景気は弱い動きが続いている。9月の製造業PMIは50.1と前月から上昇し、好不況の境目である50を3カ月ぶりに上
回った。構成指数をみると、生産は大きく上昇した一方、就業人員の低下が全体の改善ペースを抑制した。非製造業PMI
は50.2と3カ月ぶりに50を上回ったが、過去と比べると低水準で推移している。なお、2026年4～6月期の実質GDP成長
率は前年比＋4.3%と伸びが鈍化し、季節調整済み前期比（年率換算）も＋3.6%と政府目標の「4.5～5%」を下回った。

9月の米中首脳会談は一定の成果も、中心は対話継続の確認
9月24日、米国・ワシントンで米中首脳会談が実施された。5月の会談で打ち出された「建設的戦略安定関係」の構築を再
確認したほか、年内あと2回の会談の可能性が示唆されるなど、首脳間の対話の継続が確認された（下図）。
具体的には、米中双方が300億ドル相当の品目で関税引き下げに合意したほか、AI分野で対話枠組みを構築する方針で
合意するなど、個別分野では一定の進展が得られた。もっとも、総じてみると内容は概ね想定内であり、米中対立の全面
的な解消には至っていない。貿易戦争の休戦も今年11月までとされた期限が2カ月延長されたにとどまっており、今後も
両者間で対話を継続することを確認したことが最大の成果だったといえよう。

輸出価格は上昇も、デフレ輸出への懸念はなお強い
中国の輸出価格が足元で上昇している（左図）。2023年以降、EVや太陽光パネル、鉄鋼を中心に輸出価格は下落傾向
にあり、廉価品の輸出が海外市場を席巻する「デフレ輸出」が国際問題となったが、2026年4月以降、状況が一変した。品
目別でみると、世界的に需給が逼迫している集積回路やPC、PC部品の価格上昇が顕著である。
もっとも、足元の価格上昇は一部の品目に偏っている。HSコード4桁で分類した全1,204品目のうち、足元でも5割近くで価
格が下落している（右図）。このため、デフレ輸出への懸念は依然として根強い。

3Q26 獲利優於本中心預期 12%，4Q26 上修獲利 10% 
3Q26 營收 15.6 億元，季增 28%且年增 183%，毛利率 58.6%優於原估計
1.8 百分點，故獲利優於本中心 /市場預期 12%/5%，EPS 6.10 元，年增
528%；優於預期主因毛利率受到下列因素影響； 1) E -Beam 新設備產能順
利量產； 2) 上游基板來源無虞，已簽定 2 家日系及 1 家美系供貨合約。
4Q26 營收上修估計 7%至 17 億元，季增 9%且年增 165%，獲利上修 10%
至 EPS 6.7 元；上修獲利因再有 NIL(奈米壓印)新設備到位而出貨提升。 
在手訂單已達 2026 全年營收，產品數量及規格持續升級 
聯亞於 CW Laser 具技術優勢，在手訂單已達 2026 全年營收；產品包含
400G 及 800G 應用且 1.6T 已開始出貨；且 200G PD 亦已通過認證且規劃
產能；應用於 NPO/CPO 之 3.2T ELS 已與客戶共同開發。CW Laser 於市
場需求持續緊俏，未來若出貨 300mW 產品將較目前主流 70-100mW 尺寸再
大 50-70%，使供需更吃緊，聯亞董事會已通過 80 億元資本支出為 2027 年
及 2028 年新增產能佈局。 
產能擴充因應客戶長約及 AI 趨勢，正面看待營運展望 
因應美系客戶四年長約及 AI 發展趨勢，2027 年底產能將增加 150%且 2028
年底產能再增加 150%，產能為原始之 6 倍，目前新建置產能量產順利。本
中心估計 2026/2027 年營收年增 145%/108%，上修 4.6%/6.7%；EPS 為
20.5 元/48.4 元，年增 388%/136%，上修 6.7%/3.0%。今年股價本益比為
40-85 倍，且估計 2028 年-2030 年營運持續大幅成長；本中心採 2027 年估
計 EPS 以 68 倍目標本益比(與前次採 68 倍本益比相同)給予目標價 3,300 元，
評等為買進。

元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 EPS 6.1 元，季增 33%且優於本中心預期 12%，因新產能量產
順利使毛利率優於預期。上修 4Q26 預估 EPS 10%至 6.7 元。 
⧫  
在手訂單超越 2026 全年營收；董事會已通過 80 億元資本支出，產能
規劃至 2028 年底為原產能之 6 倍因應客戶四年長約及 AI 需求。 
⧫  
估 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 20.5 元/48.4 元，年增 388%/136%，獲利上
修 7%/3%；以 2027 年估計 EPS 及 68 倍 PE 得出目標價。

營運分析
3Q26 獲利優於預期 12%，4Q26 上修獲利 10%因新產能及微影製程逐步到位
3Q26 營收季年增 28%/183%，獲利優於本中心 /市場預期 12%/5%，EPS 6.1 元，季增 /年增
33%/528%；優於預期主因毛利率 58.6%優於原估計 1.8 百分點，毛利率正面影響因素為：1) 新產
能量產順利，新增加 E-Beam 設備於 7 月到位；2) 上游 InP 基板來源無虞，已簽定合約包含二家
日商及一家美商； 3) 產品組合中的高端產品比重拉升， 1.6T 產品開始放量出貨。 4Q26 預期將有
NIL(奈米壓印)微影設備到位，本中心上修營收 7%至 17 億元，季增/年增 9%/165%；毛利率上修
2.3 百分點至 59.1%；故獲利上修 10%至 EPS 6.70 元，季增/年增 10%/259%。
董事會 8 月通過 30 億元資本支出為 2028 年佈局，規劃未來二年產能達 6 倍

聯亞董事會於 8 月通過 30 億元資本支出為 2028 年佈局，加計先前通過的資本支出案共 80 億元，
規劃產能包含 MOCVD 及微影設備(E-Beam, NIL, DUV) 。規劃 2027 年底產能達 2026 年底的 2.5
倍；2028 年底再 2.5 倍達原產能的 6 倍，因應客戶未來的高速光通訊需求。聯亞與美系客戶簽定
2027-2030 年的未來四年銷售合約，因美系客戶為了防止  AI 傳輸相關零組件缺貨，提前鎖定產能。
產能因應客戶需求擴增且順暢，上修 2026 年及 2027 年獲利預估
聯亞主軸產品為 CW Laser ，1.6T 應用已量產且 3.2T 及 CPO/NPO 用之 CW Laser 持續開發，
NPO 應用有機會於 2H27 出貨；因應客戶需求已有部份 Datacom 應用之 EML Laser( 單顆 100G、
200G)出貨，200G PD 亦通過驗證且規劃部份產能。公司在手訂單已超過 2026 年營收總額，本中
心上修 2026/2027 年營收 4.6%/ 6.7% 至 53.9 億/111.9 億元，年增 145%/108% ，毛利率

57.8%/60.6%，EPS 20.5 元/48.4 元，年增 388%/136%，上修 6.7%/3.0%。

聯亞生產 CW Laser 主要為多波長產品，較單波長產品的製程更複雜 
多波長與單波長連續波雷射（ CW Laser ）的核心差異在於發射的光波頻率（顏色 /波長）數量，單
波長僅有一個固定波長；多波長包含兩個或以上的相異波長。單波長技術成熟、市面供應商多；多
波長涉及多頻率控制，設計與製造難度較高，可透過分波多工 (WDM)技術大幅提升資料傳輸率 (如
支援 1.6T、3.2T 等高階光模組）。在光柵結構與微影製程部份(Grating & Lithography) ，單波長產
品只需一次標準的磊晶生長，故微影設備使用 Stepper 即可量產；多波長產品一顆晶圓或晶粒 (Die) 
上產生不同的波長，磊晶時必須使用不對稱量子井，或透過選擇性區域磊晶 (SAE, Selective Area 
Epitaxy)技術，故多採用高成本、速度慢的電子束（E-beam）直接寫入。 
圖 3：近二年營收逐步走高，毛利率由 1Q24 的 6%提升至 1Q26/2Q26/3Q26 的 55%/58%/59%

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 10 月 29 日 LandMark 第4 頁，共 14頁  
產業概況  
光收發模組明顯成長且 800G 及 1.6T 零件更高階；NPO/CPO 數量低但高成長 
AI 對高速網路傳輸速率的需求持續攀升，光收發模組 (Optical Transceiver) 扮演關鍵角色；光收發
模組於 400G 以下且市場穩定；然而 400G 以上估年增 2026/2027 年為 80%/43%至 1 億/1.45 億個；
2026 年主流為 400G，2027 及 2028 年主流為 800G 且未來升級至 1.6T 以上則採更高階零組件，
如 Interposer、TEC 及 Submount 等電子級精密陶瓷。NPO/CPO 仍為初始階段，2026/2027/2028
年估 40 萬/320 萬/2,200 萬個：2027/2028 年增 680%/595%，惟數量遠低於光收發模組；CPO 將
OE 與交換機的 ASIC 共同封裝，降低傳輸功耗至 1/3；NPO 僅將 OE 與 ASIC 放置在交換機內同一
PCB 基板上，降低交換機傳輸功耗至 50%；二者均需插拔式 ELSFP 模組。 
圖 4：光收發模組(Optical Transceiver)市場預估數量成長；CPO/NPO 市場仍小但成長性高

插拔式光收發模組以光晶片、PCB 及 DSP 最短缺 
插拔式光收發模組以光晶片、 PCB 及 DSP 最短缺；內部架構以 DSP 為主晶片，其與 TIA 及
Driver 組成 EIC；PIC 則包含 PD、 LD、調變器； OE(光引擎)包含 EIC 與 PIC，所有零件在傳輸
速率提升下持續升級。PCB 在高階光模組需 mSAP 製程具門檻， DSP 由國際少數廠商寡占使供需
相當緊俏，內部的發射及接收端光元件晶粒以國際大廠為主。 
圖 5：光收發模組零件供應商分工架構(左)；光晶片上下游供應商以大廠為主(右)

圖 6：光收發模組供應商、以插拔式光模組為主(左)，CPO/NPO 市場仍小但成長性高(右)

資料來源：網路資料、元大投顧整理

圖 7：光收發模組中的 OE (左)；CPO/NPO 將 OE 置交換機內與 ASIC 共同封裝/同一 PCB 板 (右)

資料來源：市場資料、元大投顧整理

資料傳輸更高速使光晶片數量及規格提升；CPO/NPO 亦需插拔式光模組 
AI 交換機(Switch) 單埠（Port）的傳輸資料量主要取決於交換機的世代技術， 2026 年主流為單
Port 800G 的傳輸速率採 8 通道且單埠每秒可傳輸約 100 GB 的資料；1.6T（1.6 Tbps ）採 8 通道
且單埠每秒可傳輸達 200 GB 的資料；未來若發展 3.2T 將採 8 通道且單埠每秒可傳輸達 400 GB 
的資料。 
此外，CPO（Co-Packaged Optics ；共同封裝光學）光子積體電路（ PIC）與電子積體電路（ EIC）
共同封裝在同一個載板上，目前市場的 CPO/NPO 之 AI 交換機(Switch)，採 ELSFP（External 
Laser Small Form Pluggable ，外接雷射光源模組），調變晶片靠近 ASIC 位置以縮短傳輸距離及
降低功耗；ELSFP 將容易受温度影響且壽命較短的雷射光源獨立出來，放在主機板邊緣作為標準
化插拔模組，不僅提升散熱效率，也解決 CPO/NPO 系統的維護難題。

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 10 月 29 日 LandMark 第6 頁，共 14頁
獲利調整與股票評價
技術領先市場且獲客戶青睞簽訂長約，預期產能逐步擴增且營運展望正向
聯亞因應客戶需求，新產能建置規劃將使 2027 年及 2028 年底產能分別再提升 150%及 150%，
2028 年底為原始產能之 6 倍；近期因新建置機器設備順利且良率優於預期而使 3Q26 毛利率升至
58.6%，由於新產能運作順暢且下游客戶與上游原料供應商簽定長約而未來 2 年無虞，本中心上修
2026/2027 年營收 4.6%/6.7%，毛利率獲利上修 6.7%/3.0%，EPS 為 20.5 元/48.4 元；2027 年毛
利率較前次預估微幅下修主因法說會提及折舊費用與直接人工費用將同步提升 。本中心採 2027 年
估計 EPS 以 68 倍目標本益比(與前次相同倍數)給予目標價 3,300 元。

公司簡介  
以 MOCVD 磊晶為核心技術的Ⅲ-Ⅴ族化合物半導體廠，矽光應用為成長動能 
聯亞營運總部位於台灣南科，為 Ⅲ-Ⅴ族化合物半導體 MOCVD 磊晶廠，產品包含磷化銦 (INP)、砷化鎵(GaAs)磊晶及晶粒，
應用以矽光(Datacom)及通訊(Telecom)為主，屬於光主動元件。成長動能來自矽光應用的光收發模組 (Optical Transceivers)
之上游 PD (Photodetector) 及 LD (Laser Diode) ，聯亞積極與國際大廠合作開發資料數據中心使用的 100G、400G、800G、
1.6T 之高速模組的雷射磊晶片。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 聯亞整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於高風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於略為落後的
位置，在半導體行業中的公司中排名亦略為落後同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 聯亞的整體曝險屬於中等水準，約等於半導體行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的  ESG 議題
包含人力資本、商業道德和資源利用。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：聯亞採取了一些舉措來管理與重大 ESG 問題相關的風險，但是，公司缺乏關鍵領域
的政策和計劃。儘管如此，該公司尚未捲入任何財務方面的重大爭議。

主要負責撰寫本研究報告全文或部分內容之分析師，茲針對本報告所載證券或證券發行機構，於此聲明： (1) 文中所述觀點皆準確反映其個人對各證券或
